- **noony-complete-dual-entry**: The PRODUCTION PATTERN for Noony projects. Use when building a new project or graduating from `noony-create-fastify-server`. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/complete-dual-entry.md))
- **noony-convert-cloud-functions-to-fastify**: Use when MIGRATING existing Cloud Functions handlers to run locally with Fastify. Converts tightly-coupled Cloud Functions code into framework-agnostic handlers with dual deployment. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/convert-cloud-functions-to-fastify.md))
- **noony-create-fastify-server**: Use when setting up a Fastify server for local development from scratch. This is the STARTING POINT — get a dev server running fast, then graduate to `noony-complete-dual-entry` for production dual-entry. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/create-fastify-server.md))
- **noony-custom-adapter**: Use when creating adapters for unsupported frameworks (Koa, Hapi, NestJS), OR when a Fastify route needs access to raw request data (rawBody, custom headers) that createFastifyHandler does not expose. The executeGeneric + custom adapter pattern is the correct solution for both cases. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/custom-adapter.md))
- **noony-dependency-initialization**: Use when initializing database connections, setting up singleton services, implementing the three-condition guard for concurrent-safe one-time initialization, connecting to external APIs at startup, or preventing duplicate DB connections during cold starts in Noony handlers. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/dependency-initialization.md))
- **noony-dependency-injection**: Use when resolving services with getService(), managing ContainerPool scopes (global vs local), configuring DependencyInjectionMiddleware, understanding the hybrid proxy container memory model, or accessing type-safe services in Noony controllers. Covers service RESOLUTION, not initialization. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/dependency-injection.md))
- **noony-error-handling**: Use when throwing errors, handling error types, mapping errors to HTTP status codes, wrapping external API errors, or implementing custom error classes in Noony handlers. Covers the full error class hierarchy, cause chaining, ErrorHandlerMiddleware lifecycle, and ServiceError for non-HTTP contexts. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/error-handling.md))
- **noony-guard-system**: Use when implementing authorization, restricting endpoints by permissions, setting up role-based access control (RBAC), checking user permissions, configuring RouteGuards, using GuardSetup presets, implementing ownership-based or team-based access, or adding wildcard/complex permission expressions in Noony handlers. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/guard-system.md))
- **noony-middleware-development**: Use when creating custom middleware, adding cross-cutting concerns, intercepting requests or responses, implementing before/after/onError lifecycle hooks, passing data between middlewares via businessData, or accessing DI services inside middleware. PREREQUISITE — read `noony-middleware-ordering` first for where to place your middleware, then come here for how to build it. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/middleware-development.md))
- **noony-middleware-ordering**: THE canonical reference for middleware chain order — all other pipeline skills defer to this. ([javierhbr/random-poc](https://github.com/javierhbr/random-poc/blob/HEAD/development/claude-sample/plugins/noony-framework/commands/middleware-ordering.md))

- **notion:bootstrap**: One-shot Notion integration setup for a repo — resolves (or creates) the parent page, runs /notion:setup to scaffold canonical databases, and writes .notion/config.json so future /notion:publish / /notion:audit calls don't need to be told the parent or resolve databases by title every run. Idempotent — re-running updates the config without recreating databases. ([kevinvwong/stack-agents](https://github.com/kevinvwong/stack-agents/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/notion-bootstrap.md) · ★1)
- **notion:import**: Read a Notion page or database into the current session as context for downstream agents. Use to pull a PRD into the product agent, a research report into a usability-testing pass, or a sprint roster from a previous run. Read-only — never writes to Notion. ([kevinvwong/stack-agents](https://github.com/kevinvwong/stack-agents/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/notion-import.md) · ★1)
- **notion:promote-to-repo**: Promote a Notion-drafted artifact back to a canonical repo file. The inverse of /notion:publish — reads a Notion page, renders to markdown matching the artifact type's conventions, writes to docs/&lt;type&gt;/&lt;slug&gt;.md, flips the Notion page's Source URL to point at the new repo file. Closes the integration loop for content that originated in Notion. ([kevinvwong/stack-agents](https://github.com/kevinvwong/stack-agents/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/notion-promote-to-repo.md) · ★1)
- **notion:publish**: Publish an agent, panel, or sprint output to its canonical Notion database. Idempotent — upserts by `Source` URL so re-running updates the existing page instead of creating a duplicate. Supported artifact types: sprint, prd, research, analytics, github-audit, quality-audit, game-design, runbook. ([kevinvwong/stack-agents](https://github.com/kevinvwong/stack-agents/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/notion-publish.md) · ★1)
- **notion:setup**: Bootstrap the canonical Notion workspace layout for stack-agents — creates the Sprints, PRDs, Research, Analytics specs, GitHub audits, Quality audits, Game design docs databases and their default views. Non-destructive — skips databases that already exist (matched by title within the target parent). ([kevinvwong/stack-agents](https://github.com/kevinvwong/stack-agents/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/notion-setup.md) · ★1)

- **npl-update-changelog-docs**: `CHANGELOG.md` at the **project/sub-project root** (NOT under `docs/`) records what changed, grouped into **milestones** rather than versions — this monorepo doesn't version components independently, so meaningful clusters of change get named milestones anchored to **git tags** at their historical boundary commits. ([noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo](https://github.com/noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo/blob/HEAD/commands/npl-update-changelog-docs.md) · ★13)
- **npl-update-faq-docs**: `docs/PROJ-FAQ.md` holds **anticipated questions and honest answers** — the *why/when/compared-to-what* questions a user forms before or while adopting the component. HOWTO covers *how*; FAQ covers *why would I*, *when shouldn't I*, and *what's the catch*. ([noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo](https://github.com/noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo/blob/HEAD/commands/npl-update-faq-docs.md) · ★13)
- **npl-update-howto-docs**: `docs/PROJ-HOWTO.md` provides **task-oriented guides for the things a user will actually need to do** with this project or component. Where PROJ-ARCH explains *what it is* and PROJ-LAYOUT *where things live*, HOWTO answers *"how do I…?"* for the likely tasks — with runnable steps. ([noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo](https://github.com/noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo/blob/HEAD/commands/npl-update-howto-docs.md) · ★13)
- **npl-update-state-of-the-union-docs**: `docs/PROJ-SOTU.md` is a **dated state-of-the-union snapshot** of the component: where it currently is, what works great, what has improved since the last major cluster of changes, what it still needs, and the opportunities and threats ahead — including positioning/marketing notes. It is the document a stakeholder reads to decide *invest, use, or wait*. ([noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo](https://github.com/noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo/blob/HEAD/commands/npl-update-state-of-the-union-docs.md) · ★13)
- **npl-update-subs-doc**: `docs/SUBS.md` maps the repo's **git submodules and subtrees**: path, purpose, stack, and coupling (which other modules it depends on or interacts with). It lets agents and developers answer "where does X live and what does it touch" without opening each repo. ([noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo](https://github.com/noizu-labs-ml/NoizuPromptLingo/blob/HEAD/commands/npl-update-subs-doc.md) · ★13)

- **nxs.close**: Relay to `nexus close`, the plain command that closes an epic over its merged pull requests. Runs only when its arguments name the epic — `--epic &lt;N&gt;`, a bare `&lt;N&gt;`, or `--pr &lt;ref&gt;`, which close resolves to its epic; otherwise it refuses at once and names `nexus close --epic &lt;N&gt;`. - **nyann:apply**: Apply an Infrastructure-as-Code change — the highest-stakes mutator in nyann; it can change real cloud infrastructure. Re-runs the plan, shows it, confirms, then applies. Unmistakably opt-in: apply is never the default and destructive applies require a second explicit confirm. For IaC apply intent only (not "apply a patch", "apply formatting", or DB migrations). ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/apply.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:bootstrap**: Explicit entry point for nyann's bootstrap flow. Does exactly what the natural-language trigger ("set up this project") does, but skips intent detection so users who prefer slash commands can opt in directly. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/bootstrap.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:branch**: Create a strategy-compliant git branch. Same flow as the natural-language `new-branch` skill — dispatches here when users prefer explicit slash commands. Accepts positional args for purpose + slug so `/nyann:branch feature login` works directly without the skill having to parse phrasing. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/branch.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:check-prereqs**: Survey the machine and report which nyann features are usable right now. Classifies requirements as hard (nyann won't run without them) vs soft (feature-gated; graceful skip with a clear reason). Read-only; never installs anything. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/check-prereqs.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:cleanup-branches**: Prune local branches whose work is already merged into the base. Lists candidates first, then applies on `--yes`. Mirrors the safe-delete semantics of `git branch -d` (lowercase d): nothing unmerged is touched. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/cleanup-branches.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:commit**: Generate a Conventional Commits message from the staged diff and commit after confirmation. Same flow as the natural-language `commit` skill — dispatches here so users who prefer explicit slash commands can bypass intent detection. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/commit.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:diagnose**: Bundle a redacted support-grade snapshot of the current nyann state (state + drift + hooks + config + prereqs) for inclusion in a bug report. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/diagnose.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:diff-profile**: Compare two nyann profiles side-by-side and show what changes between them: hooks, branching, CI, documentation, extras, governance. Useful before switching profiles to understand the impact. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/diff-profile.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:doctor**: Run nyann's read-only hygiene + documentation audit on the current repo. Emits the same drift sections retrofit uses, but never offers remediation or writes to the filesystem. Use this on session start, in CI, or any time you want to sanity-check a repo without risking mutations. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/doctor.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:explain-diff**: Translate a DriftReport JSON (from doctor / retrofit / compute-drift) into a plain-English markdown narrative. Read-only template render with no LLM call — produces paste-friendly output for PR bodies, chat threads, or bug reports. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/explain-diff.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:explain-state**: Print a read-only summary of what nyann sees: stack, profile, branching, hooks, CLAUDE.md status, recent commits. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/explain-state.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:fleet**: Manage the fleet registry of local repos and show the cross-repo health dashboard — per-repo score, trend sparkline, drift counts, worst category, sorted worst-first. Read-only: it audits and reports, never remediates (that's /nyann:fleet-retrofit). ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/fleet.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:fleet-retrofit**: Bulk remediation across the fleet registry: classify every repo (clean / drifted / ungoverned / error), then walk them worst-first fixing drift — and, with --bootstrap-missing, bootstrapping un-governed repos — with a preview and an explicit confirmation PER REPO. There is no "yes to all"; each repo writes its own BootRecord and stays individually undoable. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/fleet-retrofit.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:gen-ci**: Generate a GitHub Actions CI workflow (.github/workflows/ci.yml) from the project's nyann profile and detected stack. Produces lint, typecheck, and test jobs matching the hook configuration. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/gen-ci.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:gen-claudemd**: Regenerate the nyann-managed block in CLAUDE.md from the current profile and stack detection without running a full bootstrap. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/gen-claudemd.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:gen-dependency-updater**: &gt; ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/gen-dependency-updater.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:gen-devcontainer**: Scaffold a `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` keyed to the project's primary language. Preview-by-default; idempotent on apply (refuses to overwrite a diverged config without `--force-overwrite`). ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/gen-devcontainer.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:gen-templates**: Generate GitHub PR and issue templates (.github/) from the project's nyann profile. PR template includes quality checklists matching hook configuration; issue templates provide structured bug reports and feature requests. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/gen-templates.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:gh-protect**: Audit or apply GitHub branch protection, tag rulesets, signing requirements, and repo settings based on the active profile. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/gh-protect.md) · ★7)
- **nyann:hotfix**: Create the branch topology for a patch release against a previously tagged version. Ensures `release/&lt;major&gt;.&lt;minor&gt;` exists from the source tag, then creates `hotfix/&lt;slug&gt;` off it. After this, the user commits the fix and runs `/nyann:release` from the hotfix branch. ([thettwe/nyann](https://github.com/thettwe/nyann/blob/HEAD/commands/hotfix.md) · ★7)
